\part{The ABCD dataset}

\hypertarget{overview}{%
\chapter{Overview}\label{overview}}

The Adolescent Brain Cognitive Development (ABCD) study is a long term
study of pediatric development tracking patients from 21 research sites
across the United States.

\hypertarget{mild-traumatic-brain-injuries}{%
\section{Mild traumatic brain
injuries}\label{mild-traumatic-brain-injuries}}

Four studies have previously investigated mTBI using the ABCD dataset.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Guberman et al., 2022\textsuperscript{72}
\item
  Cook and Iversion, 2021\textsuperscript{73}
\item
  Cook, Karr, Iverson, 2022\textsuperscript{74}
\item
  Dufour et al., 2020\textsuperscript{75}
\end{itemize}

The TBI information of the ABCD dataset is split across a few files:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{abcd\_otbi01.txt}: the raw, modified Ohio State TBI screen
  given at baseline
\item
  \texttt{abcd\_tbi01.txt}: summary scores of abcd\_otbi01.txt
\item
  \texttt{abcd\_lpohstbi01.txt}: all longitudinal follow-ups of the
  baseline screen
\item
  \texttt{abcd\_lsstbi01.txt}: summary scores of abcd\_lpohstbi01.txt
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{sec-abcdneuroimaging}{%
\section{Neuroimaging}\label{sec-abcdneuroimaging}}

Over the ABCD study's ten-year period of data collection, participants
are scheduled to undergo neuroimaging every 2 years. The details of this
neuroimaging are described in detail in a guide by Hagler et
al.~(\textsuperscript{76}\#\#Hagler\_Etal\_2019). This chapter provides
an easier-to-digest summary of that information.

\hypertarget{types-of-imaging-data-collected}{%
\subsection{Types of imaging data
collected}\label{types-of-imaging-data-collected}}

Three major types of imaging data are provided by the NDA:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  structural MRI (sMRI)
\item
  diffusion MRI (dMRI)
\item
  functional MRI (fMRI)

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    task-related analysis of functional MRI (task-fMRI)
  \item
    functional connectivity analysis of resting-state fMRI (rsfMRI)
  \end{itemize}
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{parcellations-of-the-brain}{%
\subsection{Parcellations of the
brain}\label{parcellations-of-the-brain}}

Detailed lists of the ROIs are present in Supplementary File 2
of\textsuperscript{76}\#\#Hagler\_Etal\_2019. Below is a list of the
different parcellation schemes that have been used for the neuroimaging
data in ABCD. Table numbers are referencing tables in Supplementary File
2 of\textsuperscript{76}.

\hypertarget{parcellations-containing-white-matter-parcels}{%
\subsubsection{Parcellations containing white matter
parcels}\label{parcellations-containing-white-matter-parcels}}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  \textbf{ASEG}: \textbf{Subcortical regions} labeled by automated
  segmentation (Fischl et al.,
  2002\textsuperscript{77}\#\#Fischl\_Etal\_2002; supp. table 1)

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    The paper itself refers to whole brain segmentation
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{APARC-DESIKAN}: \textbf{Cortical regions} (including regions
  of white matter sub-adjacent to cortical regions) defined by
  anatomical parcellation (Desikan; supp. table 2)
\item
  \textbf{ATLASTRACK}: \textbf{Fiber tracts} automatically labeled by
  AtlasTrack (Hagler; supp. table 6)
\end{enumerate}

\hypertarget{parcellations-not-containing-white-matter-parcels}{%
\subsubsection{Parcellations not containing white matter
parcels}\label{parcellations-not-containing-white-matter-parcels}}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{3}
\item
  \textbf{APARC-DESTRIEUX}: \textbf{Cortical regions} (Destrieux; supp.
  table 3)
\item
  \textbf{FUZZY}: Fuzzy-cluster parcellations based on genetic
  correlation of surface area (Chen; supp. table 4)
\item
  \textbf{GORDON}: Cortical parcels and network membership defined by
  resting-state functional connectivity (Gordon; supp. table 5)
\end{enumerate}

\hypertarget{functional-mri}{%
\subsection{Functional MRI}\label{functional-mri}}

\hypertarget{resting-state-fmri}{%
\subsubsection{Resting-state fMRI}\label{resting-state-fmri}}

\hypertarget{summary-of-all-rsfmri-files-containing-data}{%
\paragraph{Summary of all rsfMRI files containing
data:}\label{summary-of-all-rsfmri-files-containing-data}}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  ABCD rsfmri Gordon Network Correlations (abcd\_betnet02.txt)

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    based on putative cortical area parcellations defined by Gordon et
    al.~in 2016\textsuperscript{78}\#\#Gordon\_Etal\_2016
  \item
    contains the following variables:

    \begin{enumerate}
    \def\labelenumii{\alph{enumii}.}
    \tightlist
    \item
      a few variables on frame information (e.g., rotation,
      displacement; rsfmri\_c\_ngd\_tr:rsfmri\_c\_ngd\_maxrot)
    \item
      average correlation between various networks and other networks
      (rsfmri\_c\_ngd\_ad\_ngd\_ad:rsfmri\_c\_ngd\_vs\_ngd\_vs)
    \end{enumerate}
  \end{itemize}
\item
  ABCD rsfMRI Destrieux (abcd\_mrirsfd01.txt)

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    contains the following variables:

    \begin{enumerate}
    \def\labelenumii{\alph{enumii}.}
    \tightlist
    \item
      temporal variance in Destrieux ROIs (mrirsfd*)
    \end{enumerate}
  \end{itemize}
\item
  ABCD rsfMRI Network to Subcortical ROI Correlations (mrirscor02.txt)

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    contains the following variables:

    \begin{enumerate}
    \def\labelenumii{\alph{enumii}.}
    \tightlist
    \item
      a few variables on frame information (e.g., rotation,
      displacement;
      rsfmri\_cor\_ngd\_scs\_tr:rsfmri\_cor\_ngd\_scs\_maxrot)
    \item
      average correlation between various networks and ASEG ROIs
      (rsfmri\_cor\_ngd\_*)
    \end{enumerate}
  \end{itemize}
\item
  ABCD rsfMRI Temporal Variance (abcd\_mrirstv02.txt)

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    contains the following variables:

    \begin{enumerate}
    \def\labelenumii{\alph{enumii}.}
    \tightlist
    \item
      a few variables on frame information (e.g., rotation,
      displacement; rsfmri\_var\_tr:rsfmri\_var\_maxrot)
    \item
      temporal variance in APARC (APARC-DESIKAN) ROIs
      (rsmfri\_var\_cdk\_*)
    \item
      temporal variance in Gordon cortical parcellations
      (rsfmri\_var\_cortgordon\_*)
    \item
      temporal variance in ASEG ROIs (rsfmri\_var\_scs\_*)
    \end{enumerate}
  \end{itemize}
\item
  Resting-State fMRI Survey (restsurv01.txt)

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    A survey following the fMRI asking about the experience
  \end{itemize}
\end{enumerate}

\hypertarget{rsfmri-measures-included-in-snf}{%
\paragraph{rsfMRI measures included in
SNF}\label{rsfmri-measures-included-in-snf}}

We examine the following rsfMRI measures in this project: - Gordon
network correlations (\textbf{1b}) - This parcellation covers the entire
cortical surface and has been reported to have improved intra-parcel
BOLD homogeneity over the
alternatives\textsuperscript{78\#\#Gordon\_Etal\_2016,79,80} -
Subcortical correlations (\textbf{3b}) - This is the only parcellation
containing subcortical information - Gordon and subcortical temporal
variances (\textbf{4c} and \textbf{4d}) - Temporal variances have been
found to associate with outcomes in mTBI and other neurological
disorders\textsuperscript{81\#\#Dretsch\_Etal\_2019,82}

\hypertarget{structural-mri}{%
\subsection{Structural MRI}\label{structural-mri}}

This project is interested in three aspects of structural MRI: -
cortical surface areas - cortical thickness(es) - subcortical volumes

\hypertarget{summary-of-all-smri-files-containing-data}{%
\subsubsection{Summary of all sMRI files containing
data:}\label{summary-of-all-smri-files-containing-data}}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  ABCD sMRI Destrieux Parcellation

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Part 1 (\texttt{abcd\_mrisdp10201.txt})

    \begin{itemize}
    \tightlist
    \item
      Cortical thickness
    \item
      Sulcal depth
    \item
      Cortical area
    \item
      Cortical volume
    \end{itemize}
  \item
    Part 2 (\texttt{abcd\_mrisdp20201.txt})
  \item
    Part 3 (\texttt{abcd\_mrisdp30201.txt})
  \end{itemize}
\item
  ABCD sMRI

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Part 1 (\texttt{abcd\_smrip10201.txt})
  \end{itemize}
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{subcortical-volumes}{%
\subsubsection{Subcortical volumes}\label{subcortical-volumes}}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The relevant file is \texttt{abcd\_smrip10201.txt}, which contains the
  volumes of 46 subcortical regions partitioned by ASEG

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    variables \texttt{smri\_vol\_subcortaseg\_*} where \texttt{*} ranges
    from \texttt{cerebralwhitematterlh} to
    \texttt{subcorticalgrayvolume}
  \end{itemize}
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{cortical-surface-area}{%
\subsubsection{Cortical surface area}\label{cortical-surface-area}}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The relevant file is \texttt{abcd\_mrisdp10201.txt}, which contains
  the cortical thickness of \textasciitilde150 regions

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    variables \texttt{mrisdp\_*}, where \texttt{*} ranges from 303 to
    453
  \end{itemize}
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{cortical-thickness}{%
\subsubsection{Cortical thickness}\label{cortical-thickness}}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The relevant file is \texttt{abcd\_mrisdp10201.txt}, which contains
  the cortical thickness of \textasciitilde150 regions

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    variables \texttt{mrisdp\_*}, where \texttt{*} ranges from 1 to 151
  \end{itemize}
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{other-smri-measures}{%
\subsubsection{Other sMRI measures}\label{other-smri-measures}}

Measures that are not a part of the subtyping analyses, but are listed
for reference.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Cortical volumes

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    The relevant file is abcd\_mrisdp10201, which contains the cortical
    volumes of \textasciitilde150 regions

    \begin{itemize}
    \tightlist
    \item
      variables \texttt{mrisdp\_*} where * ranges from 454 to 604
    \end{itemize}
  \end{itemize}
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{diffusion-mri}{%
\subsection{Diffusion MRI}\label{diffusion-mri}}

RSI and DTI measures are both calculated for white matter fiber tract
ROIs

\hypertarget{rsi-measures}{%
\subsubsection{RSI measures}\label{rsi-measures}}

The RSI measures used in this project come from the NDA file
\texttt{mri\_rsi\_p102.txt}. The variables are all those in the form of
\texttt{dmri\_rsindwm\_cdk\_*} where \texttt{*} represents the shorthand
form of any white matter region. These variables are associated with the
Desikan parcellation.

\hypertarget{dti-measures}{%
\subsubsection{DTI measures}\label{dti-measures}}

DTI measures are provided for subcortical regions, cortical regions, and
major white matter fiber tracts.

Across all parcellations, the options for examining white matter FA and
MD are in the table below.

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\columnwidth - 6\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2190}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\columnwidth - 6\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2381}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\columnwidth - 6\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2571}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\columnwidth - 6\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2857}}@{}}
\toprule()
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Variable
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Segmentation method
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Region type
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Element name in the NDA
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule()
\endhead
Fractional anisotropy & AtlasTrack & Major WM tracts & dmdtifp1\_1 to
dmdtifp1\_35 \\
Fractional anisotropy & Anatomical parcellation & Sub-adjacent white
matter & dmdtifp1\_331 to dmdtifp1\_401 \\
Fractional anisotropy & Automated segmentation & WM of major brain
region & dmdtifp1\_211, 214, 228, 231 \\
Mean diffusivity & AtlasTrack & Major WM tracts & dmdtifp1\_43 to
dmdtifp1\_79 \\
Mean diffusivity & Anatomical parcellation & Sub-adjacent white matter &
dmdtifp1\_402 to dmdtifp1\_472 \\
Mean diffusivity & Automated segmentation & WM of major brain region &
dmdtifp1\_241, 244, 258, 261 \\
\bottomrule()
\end{longtable}

I had considered using these variables originally, but am using RSI
measures instead.

\hypertarget{sec-abcdpsych}{%
\section{Psychosocial resilience}\label{sec-abcdpsych}}

Information about psychosocial resilience is spread across several data
files in the NDA. In this project, I use the following aspects of
psychosocial resilience.

\hypertarget{parent-psychopathology}{%
\subsection{Parent psychopathology}\label{parent-psychopathology}}

Information about parent psychopathology is collected from the ``ABCD
Parent Adult Self Report Scores Aseba (ASR)'' file
(\texttt{abcd\_asrs01.txt}; category Questionnaire; release 4.0).

Psychometric scores in the ABCD study are often reported as raw scores
and as T-scores (Z score transformed to have a mean of 50 and a standard
deviation of 10). The NDA mentions that the T-scores are in relation to
norms for each gender at ages 18-35 and 36-59 (for the adult self
report). The age and gender of the parents can be obtained from the
Parent Demographics Surveys (\texttt{abcd\_lpds01.txt}; demographics
category, \texttt{pdem02.txt}; demographics category). At the time of
writing this, I am not completely sure about whether it is better to use
the raw scores or the T-scores for subtyping.

Consider the situation that there are significant differences in the raw
scores depending on the reporting parent's age group and gender. Using
the T-scores will ensure that the signal provided by this information
will be whether or not the parent's scores are abnormal for their group.
However, using the T-scores will also lose the uncorrected information
about parent gender and age that is present in the raw scores, which
could matter even more.

One way to preserve as much information as possible may be to use the
raw scores but also include demographic variables of the primary parent
(age, gender, adoptive status) into the subtyping. I will proceed with
raw scores for now and add in a subsequent section on primary parent
demographics.

\hypertarget{family-function}{%
\subsection{Family function}\label{family-function}}

Family function information is taken from the data files ``ABCD Youth
Family Environment Scale-Family Conflict Subscale Modified from PhenX
(FES)'' (\texttt{abcd\_fes01.txt}; parenting category; release 4.0) and
``ABCD Parent Family Environment Scale-Family Conflict Subscale Modified
from PhenX (FES)'' (\texttt{fes02.txt}; parenting category; release
4.0).

\hypertarget{prosocial-behaviour}{%
\subsection{Prosocial behaviour}\label{prosocial-behaviour}}

Prosocial behaviour information was originally planned to be collected
from the ``Strengths and Difficulties Questionnaire'', but the
questionnaire only contained responses from around 3000 subjects.
Instead, information is collected from the ``Youth Prosocial Behavior
Survey'' (\texttt{abcd\_psb01.txt}; behavior category; release 4.0) and
the ``Parent Prosocial Behavior Survey'' (\texttt{psb01.txt}; behavior
category; release 4.0), which ask three questions:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Is the child nice to other people and do they care about their
  feelings
\item
  Is the child helpful if someone is hurt, upset, or feeling sick
\item
  Does the child often offer to help others
\end{enumerate}

\hypertarget{loneliness}{%
\subsection{Loneliness}\label{loneliness}}

Loneliness will be tracked by factoring in the number of close friends
(\texttt{resiliency5b\_y} + \texttt{resiliency6b\_y}) and how many
friends (\texttt{resiliency5a\_y} + \texttt{resiliency6a\_y}) each
subject has from the ABCD Other Resilience file
(\texttt{abcd\_ysr01.txt}; category Social Adjustments; release 4.0).

\hypertarget{healthy-behaviours}{%
\subsection{Healthy behaviours}\label{healthy-behaviours}}

Three forms of healthy behaviour are tracked:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Screen time
\item
  Involvement in sports and activities
\item
  Exercise habits
\end{enumerate}

Screen time information is collected from the Youth Screen Time Survey
(\texttt{abcd\_stq01.txt}; category Questionnaire; release 4.0) and the
Parent Screen Time Survey (\texttt{stq01.txt}; category Questionnaire;
release 4.0). Within the youth survey, the following variables are
collected for both weekdays and weekends separately:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  hours watching TV / movies
\item
  hours watching videos
\item
  hours playing video games
\item
  hours spent texting
\item
  hours on social media
\item
  hours spent video chatting
\end{itemize}

These variables span \texttt{screen1\_wkdy\_y} to
\texttt{screen12\_wknd\_y}.

Within the parent survey, the following variables are collected for
weekdays and weekends separately, as well as for reporting in minutes
and hours separately:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  time spent on a computer
\end{itemize}

These variables span \texttt{screentime1\_p\_hours} to
\texttt{screentime2\_p\_minutes}.

\hypertarget{explore}{%
\chapter{Explore}\label{explore}}

\hypertarget{searching-for-pnc-pilot-associations-in-the-abcd-dataset}{%
\section{Searching for PNC pilot associations in the ABCD
dataset}\label{searching-for-pnc-pilot-associations-in-the-abcd-dataset}}

Dr.~Wheeler's previous SNF analyses in the PNC) identified three
subtypes. Subtype \#3 was found to have a significantly higher
proportion of children that experienced LOC following their injuries,
significantly poorer outcomes on the line orientation test (a measure of
visuospatial processing), and significantly lower functional
connectivity between the brainstem and occipital cortex.

\includegraphics[width=0.7\textwidth,height=\textheight]{abcd/../figures/abcd/pnc_aw.png}

There is no equivalent assessment of the line orientation test in the
ABCD dataset.

Within the available rs-fMRI parcellations, the occipital cortex is best
captured by the Gordon parcellation ``visual network'' and the brainstem
is best captured by the ASEG parcellation ``brain-stem''.

The variable of interest is \texttt{rsfmri\_cor\_ngd\_vs\_scs\_bs} (the
average correlation between these two regions).

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\FunctionTok{library}\NormalTok{(scabcd)}
\FunctionTok{library}\NormalTok{(readr)}

\CommentTok{\# Import baseline TBI data}
\NormalTok{otbi01 }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{read\_delim}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"abcd/4.0/trauma/abcd\_otbi01.txt"}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# Change column names to human{-}readable}
\NormalTok{otbi01\_renamed }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{rename\_tbi}\NormalTok{(otbi01)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\hypertarget{extract-and-transform}{%
\chapter{Extract and transform}\label{extract-and-transform}}

\hypertarget{subjects}{%
\section{Subjects}\label{subjects}}

All information required for identifying children at a chronic stage
following mTBI as of baseline data collection is available in the
\texttt{abcd\_otbi01.txt} file. To identify subjects at a chronic stage
post-mTBI, two intermediate pieces of information must be calculated
from the original dataset:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  identifying which head injuries were mTBIs
\item
  identifying the time since the most recent mTBI
\end{itemize}

The structure of the Ohio State TBI screen as used for ABCD data
collection was not completely compatible with the most commonly used
definition of mTBI\textsuperscript{1}, which requires at least one of
the following:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Any period of loss of consciousness
\item
  Any loss of memory
\item
  Any alteration in mental state
\item
  Focal neurological deficits
\end{itemize}

but none of the following:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  LOC over 30 minutes
\item
  A Glasgow Coma Scale score below 13
\item
  Post-traumatic amnesia (PTA) greater than 24 hours
\end{itemize}

A few aspects of the data collection process in the ABCD study precluded
the usage of this definition:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  No information was collected regarding the GCS
\item
  Feeling dazed and PTA were combined into a single variable, making it
  unclear which combination of symptoms was present
\item
  No information was collected regarding the duration of feeling dazed
  or experiencing PTA
\end{itemize}

Consequently, I defined mTBI as any injury that resulted in
\textbf{either}:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  LOC for less than 30 minutes (including 0 minutes) \textbf{AND} any
  duration of memory loss / feeling dazed
\item
  LOC for less than 30 minutes but greater than 0 minutes
\end{enumerate}

The fixed nature of the questionnaire also meant that some information
about multiple mTBIs sustained in the same way or about a very large
number of mTBIs throughout a child's life could have been lost (see the
previous section on identifying children with a history of mTBI). For my
analyses, I assume these edge cases are not common enough to
meaningfully change any obtained conclusions.

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\FunctionTok{library}\NormalTok{(ggplot2)}
\FunctionTok{library}\NormalTok{(scabcd)}
\FunctionTok{library}\NormalTok{(readr)}
\FunctionTok{library}\NormalTok{(dplyr)}
\FunctionTok{library}\NormalTok{(here)}
\FunctionTok{library}\NormalTok{(knitr)}

\CommentTok{\# Location of processed data}
\NormalTok{input\_path }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \StringTok{"data/abcd/processed/prashanth/data/inputs/"}
\NormalTok{subject\_path }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \StringTok{"data/abcd/processed/prashanth/data/subjects/"}

\CommentTok{\# Import baseline TBI dataset}
\NormalTok{otbi01 }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{read\_delim}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{here}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"data/abcd/4.0/trauma/abcd\_otbi01.txt"}\NormalTok{))}

\CommentTok{\# Identify baseline mtbi subjects at chronic stage post{-}mTBI (\textgreater{} 3 months)}
\NormalTok{mtbi\_subjects }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{get\_mtbi\_subjects}\NormalTok{(otbi01, }\AttributeTok{min\_latest\_mtbi\_mpi =} \DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
[1] "Note: This function converts the mTBI column types to numeric."
[1] "Note: Interview ages are provided in months, but injury ages are provided in years. Injury ages will be scaled up to determine time between interview and latest mTBI."
\end{verbatim}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\FunctionTok{write\_csv}\NormalTok{(mtbi\_subjects, }\FunctionTok{paste0}\NormalTok{(subject\_path, }\StringTok{"mtbi\_subjects.csv"}\NormalTok{))}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

A few types of injuries were handled differently from the rest in the
above code. For the \texttt{other\_loc} injury type, the questionnaire
asked for a total number of injuries with LOC as well as a total number
of injuries with LOC above 30 minutes. If their difference was above 0,
they were labelled as having had an mTBI. For the \texttt{other\_multi}
and \texttt{other\_other\_multi} injury types, insufficient information
was provided to determine the severity of the associated injuries.

For my analyses, I consider 3 months to be the threshold at which an
mTBI patient enters a chronic stage post-injury.

\hypertarget{acute-symptoms}{%
\section{Acute symptoms}\label{acute-symptoms}}

The acute symptom data is found within the TBI dataset. We are interesed
in the following acute symptoms:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  mechanism of injury
\item
  age
\item
  LOC information
\item
  Feeling dazed or having memory loss
\end{itemize}

A few more intermediate columns need to be added to isolate these
variables for the most recent mTBIs of the subjects.

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{as\_mtbi\_mem\_daze }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{get\_mtbi\_mem\_daze}\NormalTok{(otbi01, mtbi\_subjects)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\end{verbatim}

\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
[1] "Note: This function converts the mTBI column types to numeric."
[1] "Note: Interview ages are provided in months, but injury ages are provided in years. Injury ages will be scaled up to determine time between interview and latest mTBI."
\end{verbatim}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{as\_mtbi\_loc }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{get\_mtbi\_loc}\NormalTok{(otbi01, mtbi\_subjects)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
[1] "Note: This function converts the mTBI column types to numeric."
[1] "Note: Interview ages are provided in months, but injury ages are provided in years. Injury ages will be scaled up to determine time between interview and latest mTBI."
\end{verbatim}

\begin{Shaded}

\NormalTok{as\_mtbi\_age }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{get\_mtbi\_age}\NormalTok{(otbi01, mtbi\_subjects)}

\begin{verbatim}
[1] "Note: This function converts the mTBI column types to numeric."

\end{verbatim}

\begin{Shaded}

\FunctionTok{write\_csv}\NormalTok{(as\_mtbi\_mem\_daze, }\FunctionTok{paste0}\NormalTok{(input\_path, }\StringTok{"as\_mtbi\_mem\_daze.csv"}\NormalTok{))}
\FunctionTok{write\_csv}\NormalTok{(as\_mtbi\_mechanism, }\FunctionTok{paste0}\NormalTok{(input\_path, }\StringTok{"as\_mtbi\_mechanism.csv"}\NormalTok{))}
\FunctionTok{write\_csv}\NormalTok{(as\_mtbi\_loc, }\FunctionTok{paste0}\NormalTok{(input\_path, }\StringTok{"as\_mtbi\_loc.csv"}\NormalTok{))}

\hypertarget{demographics}{%
\section{Demographics}\label{demographics}}

Demographic variables included in the analyses are:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Age
\item
  Sex
\item
  Race
\item
  Household income
\item
  Pubertal development status
\end{itemize}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# Demographic dataframes}
\NormalTok{ssphp01 }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{read\_delim}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{here}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"data/abcd/4.0/med{-}history/abcd\_ssphp01.txt"}\NormalTok{))}
\NormalTok{ssphy01 }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{read\_delim}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{here}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"data/abcd/4.0/med{-}history/abcd\_ssphy01.txt"}\NormalTok{))}
\NormalTok{pdem02 }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{read\_delim}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{here}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"data/abcd/4.0/demographics/pdem02.txt"}\NormalTok{))}

\CommentTok{\# Pubertal status}
\NormalTok{d\_pubertal\_status }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{get\_pubertal\_status}\NormalTok{(ssphp01, ssphy01, mtbi\_subjects)}

\CommentTok{\# Age on interview}
\NormalTok{d\_interview\_age }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{get\_interview\_age}\NormalTok{(otbi01, mtbi\_subjects)}

\CommentTok{\# Age on injury}
\NormalTok{d\_mtbi\_age }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{get\_mtbi\_age}\NormalTok{(otbi01, mtbi\_subjects)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
[1] "Note: This function converts the mTBI column types to numeric."
[1] "Note: Interview ages are provided in months, but injury ages are provided in years. Injury ages will be scaled up to determine time between interview and latest mTBI."
\end{verbatim}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# Sex}
\NormalTok{d\_sex }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{get\_sex}\NormalTok{(otbi01, mtbi\_subjects)}

\CommentTok{\# Household income}
\NormalTok{d\_income }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{get\_income}\NormalTok{(pdem02, mtbi\_subjects)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

I will be trying two separate categorizations:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  White, black, mixed/other
\item
  White, black, hispanic, asian/other pacific islander, native american
\end{enumerate}

The rationale for this decision can be found at
(Chapter~\ref{sec-racebreakdown}).

\hypertarget{race}{%
\chapter{Race}\label{race}}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# CONTINUE FROM HERE!}
\NormalTok{d\_race }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{get\_race}\NormalTok{(pdem02, mtbi\_subjects, }\StringTok{"condensed\_undummied"}\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\hypertarget{medical-history}{%
\section{Medical history}\label{medical-history}}

Medical history variables of interest include:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Number of previous head injuries

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    discrete variable \texttt{mtbi\_count} from the processed file
    \texttt{abcd\_otbi01\_detailed.csv}
  \end{itemize}
\item
  History of headaches

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    binary variable \texttt{medhx\_2q} from the file ABCD Parent Medical
    History Questionnaire (\texttt{abcd\_mx01.txt}; category Med
    History; release 2.0)

    \begin{itemize}
    \tightlist
    \item
      checks if subject has ever gone to doctor for bad headaches
    \end{itemize}
  \end{itemize}
\end{enumerate}

Unfortunately, information on pain (the file \texttt{abcd\_pq01.txt}), a
factor noted as an important covariate by the original A-CAP study
(\textsuperscript{83}), was not a part of ABCD's baseline data
collection.

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{mx01 }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{read\_delim}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{here}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"data/abcd/4.0/med{-}history/abcd\_mx01.txt"}\NormalTok{))}

\CommentTok{\# History of headaches}
\NormalTok{mh\_headaches }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{get\_headaches}\NormalTok{(mx01, mtbi\_subjects)}

\CommentTok{\# Number of previous mTBIs}
\NormalTok{mh\_mtbi\_count }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{get\_mtbi\_count}\NormalTok{(otbi01, mtbi\_subjects)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
[1] "Note: This function converts the mTBI column types to numeric."
[1] "Note: Interview ages are provided in months, but injury ages are provided in years. Injury ages will be scaled up to determine time between interview and latest mTBI."
\end{verbatim}

Subset and export the medical history variables:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\FunctionTok{write\_csv}\NormalTok{(mh\_headaches, }\FunctionTok{paste0}\NormalTok{(input\_path, }\StringTok{"mh\_headaches.csv"}\NormalTok{))}
\FunctionTok{write\_csv}\NormalTok{(mh\_mtbi\_count, }\FunctionTok{paste0}\NormalTok{(input\_path, }\StringTok{"mh\_mtbi\_count.csv"}\NormalTok{))}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\hypertarget{neuroimaging}{%
\section{Neuroimaging}\label{neuroimaging}}

Variables of interest related to brain structure and function include:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Structural: T1-weighted multishell diffusion-weighted MRI data
\item
  Diffusion: Neurite density (restricted spectrum imaging)
\item
  Functional: resting-state functional MRI data
\end{itemize}

The 2019 publication by Hagler et al.\textsuperscript{76} outlines the
neuroimaging data present in the ABCD dataset in detail.

\hypertarget{structural-mri-1}{%
\subsection{Structural MRI}\label{structural-mri-1}}

The sMRI variables of interest include subcortical volumes, and cortical
thicknesses, and cortical surface areas.

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# SMRI dataframes}
\NormalTok{smrip10201 }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}
    \FunctionTok{read\_delim}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{here}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"data/abcd/4.0/evaluated{-}data/abcd\_smrip10201.txt"}\NormalTok{))}
\NormalTok{mrisdp10201 }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}
    \FunctionTok{read\_delim}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{here}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"data/abcd/4.0/evaluated{-}data/abcd\_mrisdp10201.txt"}\NormalTok{))}

\CommentTok{\# Subcortical volumes}
\NormalTok{sm\_subc\_v }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{get\_subc\_v}\NormalTok{(smrip10201, mtbi\_subjects)}

\CommentTok{\# Cortical thickness}
\NormalTok{sm\_cort\_t }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{get\_cort\_t}\NormalTok{(mrisdp10201, mtbi\_subjects)}

\CommentTok{\# Cortical volumes}
\NormalTok{sm\_cort\_sa }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{get\_cort\_sa}\NormalTok{(mrisdp10201, mtbi\_subjects)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\FunctionTok{write\_csv}\NormalTok{(sm\_subc\_v, }\FunctionTok{paste0}\NormalTok{(input\_path, }\StringTok{"sm\_subc\_v.csv"}\NormalTok{))}
\FunctionTok{write\_csv}\NormalTok{(sm\_cort\_t, }\FunctionTok{paste0}\NormalTok{(input\_path, }\StringTok{"sm\_cort\_t.csv"}\NormalTok{))}
\FunctionTok{write\_csv}\NormalTok{(sm\_cort\_sa, }\FunctionTok{paste0}\NormalTok{(input\_path, }\StringTok{"sm\_cort\_sa.csv"}\NormalTok{))}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\hypertarget{diffusion-mri-1}{%
\subsection{Diffusion MRI}\label{diffusion-mri-1}}

We are interested in the neurite density measures of white matter
regions in the brain. Previously, these measures were found in variables
containing the string ``rsindwm\_cdk'' within the file
\texttt{mri\_rsi\_p102.txt}. A change anounced in the ABCD 4.0 MRI
release notes indicates that neurite density is now referred to as
``restricted normalized directional'' and that the relevant white matter
variables contain the string ``rsirndwm'' within the file
\texttt{abcd\_drsip201.txt}.

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# DMRI dataframe}
\NormalTok{drsip201 }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}
    \FunctionTok{read\_delim}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{here}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"data/abcd/4.0/evaluated{-}data/abcd\_drsip201.txt"}\NormalTok{))}

\CommentTok{\# White matter neurite densities}
\NormalTok{dm\_wmndf }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{get\_wmnd}\NormalTok{(drsip201, mtbi\_subjects)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\FunctionTok{write\_csv}\NormalTok{(dm\_wmndf, }\FunctionTok{paste0}\NormalTok{(input\_path, }\StringTok{"dm\_wmndf.csv"}\NormalTok{))}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\hypertarget{resting-state-functional-mri}{%
\subsection{Resting-state functional
MRI}\label{resting-state-functional-mri}}

We are interested in the following rs-fMRI measures:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Gordon network (cortical) correlations
\item
  Subcortical correlations
\item
  Gordon and subcortical temporal variances
\end{itemize}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{betnet02 }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}
    \FunctionTok{read\_delim}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{here}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{paste0}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"data/abcd/2.0/tabular{-}MRI/rsfMRI{-}data/"}\NormalTok{,}
                           \StringTok{"rsfmri{-}gord{-}cor/abcd\_betnet02.txt"}\NormalTok{)))}
\NormalTok{mrirscor02 }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}
    \FunctionTok{read\_delim}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{here}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{paste0}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"data/abcd/2.0/tabular{-}MRI/rsfMRI{-}data/"}\NormalTok{,}
                           \StringTok{"rsfmri{-}subc{-}cor/mrirscor02.txt"}\NormalTok{)))}
\NormalTok{mrirstv02 }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}
    \FunctionTok{read\_delim}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{here}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{paste0}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"data/abcd/2.0/tabular{-}MRI/rsfMRI{-}data/"}\NormalTok{,}
                           \StringTok{"rsfmri{-}temp{-}var/abcd\_mrirstv02.txt"}\NormalTok{)))}


\CommentTok{\# Cortical parcellation network correlations}
\NormalTok{rm\_gord\_cor }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{get\_gord\_cor}\NormalTok{(betnet02, mtbi\_subjects)}

\CommentTok{\# Subcortical parcellation network correlations}
\NormalTok{rm\_subc\_cor }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{get\_subc\_cor}\NormalTok{(mrirscor02, mtbi\_subjects)}

\CommentTok{\# Temporal variances {-} cortical parcellations}
\NormalTok{rm\_gord\_var }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{get\_gord\_var}\NormalTok{(mrirstv02, mtbi\_subjects)}

\CommentTok{\# Subcortical temporal variances}
\NormalTok{rm\_subc\_var }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{get\_subc\_var}\NormalTok{(mrirstv02, mtbi\_subjects)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\FunctionTok{write\_csv}\NormalTok{(rm\_gord\_cor, }\FunctionTok{paste0}\NormalTok{(input\_path, }\StringTok{"rm\_gord\_cor.csv"}\NormalTok{))}
\FunctionTok{write\_csv}\NormalTok{(rm\_subc\_cor, }\FunctionTok{paste0}\NormalTok{(input\_path, }\StringTok{"rm\_subc\_cor.csv"}\NormalTok{))}
\FunctionTok{write\_csv}\NormalTok{(rm\_subc\_var, }\FunctionTok{paste0}\NormalTok{(input\_path, }\StringTok{"rm\_subc\_var"}\NormalTok{))}
\FunctionTok{write\_csv}\NormalTok{(rm\_gord\_var, }\FunctionTok{paste0}\NormalTok{(input\_path, }\StringTok{"rm\_gord\_var.csv"}\NormalTok{))}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\hypertarget{psychosocial-resilience}{%

Psychosocial factors of interest include:

  prosocial behaviour

  loneliness
\item
  healthy behaviours

  parent psychopathology
\end{itemize}

\part{Appendix}

\hypertarget{sec-racebreakdown}{%
\chapter{Race breakdown}\label{sec-racebreakdown}}

Race/ethnicity is a bit confusing to deal with. A huge fraction of
subjects identify with multiple categories. While that is no issue for
constructing similarity matrices, it is an issue for comparing clusters
by race/ethnicity post-subtyping. Different publications have handled
this differently. I am currently categorizing all subjects as either
white, black, or `mixed/other'.

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\FunctionTok{library}\NormalTok{(readr)}
\FunctionTok{library}\NormalTok{(here)}
\FunctionTok{library}\NormalTok{(dplyr)}
\FunctionTok{library}\NormalTok{(scabcd)}

\NormalTok{mtbi\_subjects }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}
    \FunctionTok{read\_csv}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{here}\NormalTok{(}
        \StringTok{"data/abcd/processed/prashanth/data/subjects/mtbi\_subjects.csv"}\NormalTok{))}

\NormalTok{pdem02 }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{read\_delim}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{here}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"data/abcd/4.0/demographics/pdem02.txt"}\NormalTok{))}

\NormalTok{full\_race\_df }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{abcd\_import}\NormalTok{(pdem02, mtbi\_subjects) }\SpecialCharTok{|\textgreater{}}
\NormalTok{    dplyr}\SpecialCharTok{::}\FunctionTok{rename}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"white"} \OtherTok{=} \StringTok{"demo\_race\_a\_p\_\_\_10"}\NormalTok{,}
           \StringTok{"black"} \OtherTok{=} \StringTok{"demo\_race\_a\_p\_\_\_11"}\NormalTok{,}
           \StringTok{"native\_american"} \OtherTok{=} \StringTok{"demo\_race\_a\_p\_\_\_12"}\NormalTok{,}
           \StringTok{"native\_alaskan"} \OtherTok{=} \StringTok{"demo\_race\_a\_p\_\_\_13"}\NormalTok{,}
           \StringTok{"native\_hawaiian"} \OtherTok{=} \StringTok{"demo\_race\_a\_p\_\_\_14"}\NormalTok{,}
           \StringTok{"guamanian"} \OtherTok{=} \StringTok{"demo\_race\_a\_p\_\_\_15"}\NormalTok{,}
           \StringTok{"samoan"} \OtherTok{=} \StringTok{"demo\_race\_a\_p\_\_\_16"}\NormalTok{,}
           \StringTok{"other\_pacific\_islander"} \OtherTok{=} \StringTok{"demo\_race\_a\_p\_\_\_17"}\NormalTok{,}
           \StringTok{"asian"} \OtherTok{=} \StringTok{"demo\_race\_a\_p\_\_\_18"}\NormalTok{,}
           \StringTok{"chinese"} \OtherTok{=} \StringTok{"demo\_race\_a\_p\_\_\_19"}\NormalTok{,}
           \StringTok{"filipino"} \OtherTok{=} \StringTok{"demo\_race\_a\_p\_\_\_20"}\NormalTok{,}
           \StringTok{"japanese"} \OtherTok{=} \StringTok{"demo\_race\_a\_p\_\_\_21"}\NormalTok{,}
           \StringTok{"korean"} \OtherTok{=} \StringTok{"demo\_race\_a\_p\_\_\_22"}\NormalTok{,}
           \StringTok{"vietnamese"} \OtherTok{=} \StringTok{"demo\_race\_a\_p\_\_\_23"}\NormalTok{,}
           \StringTok{"other\_asian"} \OtherTok{=} \StringTok{"demo\_race\_a\_p\_\_\_24"}\NormalTok{,}
           \StringTok{"other"} \OtherTok{=} \StringTok{"demo\_race\_a\_p\_\_\_25"}\NormalTok{,}
           \StringTok{"refuse\_to\_answer"} \OtherTok{=} \StringTok{"demo\_race\_a\_p\_\_\_77"}\NormalTok{,}
           \StringTok{"dont\_know"} \OtherTok{=} \StringTok{"demo\_race\_a\_p\_\_\_99"}\NormalTok{,}
           \StringTok{"hispanic"} \OtherTok{=} \StringTok{"demo\_ethn\_v2"}\NormalTok{) }\SpecialCharTok{|\textgreater{}}
\NormalTok{    dplyr}\SpecialCharTok{::}\FunctionTok{select}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"subjectkey"}\NormalTok{,}
                  \StringTok{"white"}\SpecialCharTok{:}\StringTok{"hispanic"}\NormalTok{,}
                  \SpecialCharTok{{-}}\StringTok{"demo\_race\_notes\_v2"}\NormalTok{) }\SpecialCharTok{|\textgreater{}}
\NormalTok{    dplyr}\SpecialCharTok{::}\FunctionTok{mutate}\NormalTok{(}
\NormalTok{        dplyr}\SpecialCharTok{::}\FunctionTok{across}\NormalTok{(}
            \StringTok{"white"}\SpecialCharTok{:}\StringTok{"hispanic"}\NormalTok{, as.numeric))}
\NormalTok{    full\_race\_df }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}\NormalTok{ full\_race\_df }\SpecialCharTok{|\textgreater{}}
\NormalTok{        dplyr}\SpecialCharTok{::}\FunctionTok{mutate}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{hispanic =}
\NormalTok{            dplyr}\SpecialCharTok{::}\FunctionTok{case\_when}\NormalTok{(full\_race\_df}\SpecialCharTok{$}\StringTok{"hispanic"} \SpecialCharTok{==} \DecValTok{1} \SpecialCharTok{\textasciitilde{}} \DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{, }\ConstantTok{TRUE} \SpecialCharTok{\textasciitilde{}} \DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{))}

\FunctionTok{colSums}\NormalTok{(full\_race\_df }\SpecialCharTok{|\textgreater{}} \FunctionTok{select}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialCharTok{{-}}\StringTok{"subjectkey"}\NormalTok{))}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
                 white                  black        native_american 
                   349                     65                     18 
        native_alaskan        native_hawaiian              guamanian 
                     0                      1                      0 
                samoan other_pacific_islander                  asian 
                     0                      1                      7 
               chinese               filipino               japanese 
                     4                      5                      1 
                korean             vietnamese            other_asian 
                     2                      1                      1 
                 other       refuse_to_answer              dont_know 
                    29                      3                      3 
              hispanic 
                    69 
\end{verbatim}

Several categories have less than 10 subjects in them(native alaskan,
native hawaiian, guamanian, samoan, other pacific islander, asian,
chinese, filipino, japanese, korean, vietnamese, other asian).

In the next chunk, I pool some of them together:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{full\_race\_df2 }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}\NormalTok{ full\_race\_df }\SpecialCharTok{|\textgreater{}}
\NormalTok{    dplyr}\SpecialCharTok{::}\FunctionTok{mutate}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{asian\_other\_pi =} \FunctionTok{pmax}\NormalTok{(}
\NormalTok{        full\_race\_df}\SpecialCharTok{$}\StringTok{"asian"}\NormalTok{,}
\NormalTok{        full\_race\_df}\SpecialCharTok{$}\StringTok{"filipino"}\NormalTok{,}
\NormalTok{        full\_race\_df}\SpecialCharTok{$}\StringTok{"korean"}\NormalTok{,}
\NormalTok{        full\_race\_df}\SpecialCharTok{$}\StringTok{"other\_asian"}\NormalTok{,}
\NormalTok{        full\_race\_df}\SpecialCharTok{$}\StringTok{"chinese"}\NormalTok{,}
\NormalTok{        full\_race\_df}\SpecialCharTok{$}\StringTok{"japanese"}\NormalTok{,}
\NormalTok{        full\_race\_df}\SpecialCharTok{$}\StringTok{"vietnamese"}\NormalTok{,}
\NormalTok{        full\_race\_df}\SpecialCharTok{$}\StringTok{"other\_pacific\_islander"}\NormalTok{,}
\NormalTok{        full\_race\_df}\SpecialCharTok{$}\StringTok{"samoan"}\NormalTok{,}
\NormalTok{        full\_race\_df}\SpecialCharTok{$}\StringTok{"guamanian"}\NormalTok{,}
\NormalTok{        full\_race\_df}\SpecialCharTok{$}\StringTok{"native\_hawaiian"}\NormalTok{,}
\NormalTok{        full\_race\_df}\SpecialCharTok{$}\StringTok{"native\_alaskan"}\NormalTok{),}
    \AttributeTok{na =} \FunctionTok{pmax}\NormalTok{(}
\NormalTok{        full\_race\_df}\SpecialCharTok{$}\StringTok{"dont\_know"}\NormalTok{,}
\NormalTok{        full\_race\_df}\SpecialCharTok{$}\StringTok{"refuse\_to\_answer"}\NormalTok{)) }\SpecialCharTok{|\textgreater{}}
\NormalTok{    dplyr}\SpecialCharTok{::}\FunctionTok{select}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"subjectkey"}\NormalTok{,}
           \StringTok{"asian\_other\_pi"}\NormalTok{,}
           \StringTok{"native\_american"}\NormalTok{,}
           \StringTok{"other"}\NormalTok{,}
           \StringTok{"na"}\NormalTok{,}
           \StringTok{"white"}\NormalTok{,}
           \StringTok{"black"}\NormalTok{,}
           \StringTok{"hispanic"}\NormalTok{)}

\FunctionTok{colSums}\NormalTok{(full\_race\_df2 }\SpecialCharTok{|\textgreater{}} \FunctionTok{select}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialCharTok{{-}}\StringTok{"subjectkey"}\NormalTok{))}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
 asian_other_pi native_american           other              na           white 
             22              18              29               6             349 
          black        hispanic 
             65              69 
\end{verbatim}

These seems like a sensible breakdown, but the main issue is that many
subjects belong to more than one of these categories.

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# How many subjects belong to more than one race category?}
\FunctionTok{sum}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{rowSums}\NormalTok{(full\_race\_df2 }\SpecialCharTok{|\textgreater{}} \FunctionTok{select}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialCharTok{{-}}\StringTok{"subjectkey"}\NormalTok{)) }\SpecialCharTok{\textgreater{}} \DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
[1] 110
\end{verbatim}

Quite a large number of subjects are mixed. In the next chunk, I assign
those subjects to their own `mixed' category and remove them from the
individual race categories they previously belonged to.

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{full\_race\_df3 }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}\NormalTok{ full\_race\_df2 }\SpecialCharTok{|\textgreater{}}
\NormalTok{    dplyr}\SpecialCharTok{::}\FunctionTok{mutate}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{mixed =}\NormalTok{ dplyr}\SpecialCharTok{::}\FunctionTok{case\_when}\NormalTok{(}
\NormalTok{        full\_race\_df2}\SpecialCharTok{$}\StringTok{"white"} \SpecialCharTok{+}
\NormalTok{        full\_race\_df2}\SpecialCharTok{$}\StringTok{"black"} \SpecialCharTok{+}
\NormalTok{        full\_race\_df2}\SpecialCharTok{$}\StringTok{"asian\_other\_pi"} \SpecialCharTok{+}
\NormalTok{        full\_race\_df2}\SpecialCharTok{$}\StringTok{"native\_american"} \SpecialCharTok{+}
\NormalTok{        full\_race\_df2}\SpecialCharTok{$}\StringTok{"other"} \SpecialCharTok{+}
\NormalTok{        full\_race\_df2}\SpecialCharTok{$}\StringTok{"hispanic"} \SpecialCharTok{\textgreater{}} \DecValTok{1} \SpecialCharTok{\textasciitilde{}} \DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{,}
        \ConstantTok{TRUE} \SpecialCharTok{\textasciitilde{}} \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{        ))}
\CommentTok{\# Remove original race category for those who are mixed}
\NormalTok{full\_race\_df4 }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}\NormalTok{ full\_race\_df3 }\SpecialCharTok{|\textgreater{}}
\NormalTok{    dplyr}\SpecialCharTok{::}\FunctionTok{mutate}\NormalTok{(}
        \AttributeTok{black =}\NormalTok{ dplyr}\SpecialCharTok{::}\FunctionTok{case\_when}\NormalTok{(}
\NormalTok{            full\_race\_df3}\SpecialCharTok{$}\StringTok{"black"} \SpecialCharTok{==} \DecValTok{1} \SpecialCharTok{\&}
\NormalTok{                full\_race\_df3}\SpecialCharTok{$}\StringTok{"mixed"} \SpecialCharTok{==} \DecValTok{0} \SpecialCharTok{\textasciitilde{}} \DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{,}
            \ConstantTok{TRUE} \SpecialCharTok{\textasciitilde{}} \DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{),}
        \AttributeTok{white =}\NormalTok{ dplyr}\SpecialCharTok{::}\FunctionTok{case\_when}\NormalTok{(}
\NormalTok{            full\_race\_df3}\SpecialCharTok{$}\StringTok{"white"} \SpecialCharTok{==} \DecValTok{1} \SpecialCharTok{\&}
\NormalTok{                full\_race\_df3}\SpecialCharTok{$}\StringTok{"mixed"} \SpecialCharTok{==} \DecValTok{0} \SpecialCharTok{\textasciitilde{}} \DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{,}
            \ConstantTok{TRUE} \SpecialCharTok{\textasciitilde{}} \DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{),}
        \AttributeTok{asian\_other\_pi =}\NormalTok{ dplyr}\SpecialCharTok{::}\FunctionTok{case\_when}\NormalTok{(}
\NormalTok{            full\_race\_df3}\SpecialCharTok{$}\StringTok{"asian\_other\_pi"} \SpecialCharTok{==} \DecValTok{1} \SpecialCharTok{\&}
\NormalTok{                full\_race\_df3}\SpecialCharTok{$}\StringTok{"mixed"} \SpecialCharTok{==} \DecValTok{0} \SpecialCharTok{\textasciitilde{}} \DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{,}
            \ConstantTok{TRUE} \SpecialCharTok{\textasciitilde{}} \DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{),}
        \AttributeTok{hispanic =}\NormalTok{ dplyr}\SpecialCharTok{::}\FunctionTok{case\_when}\NormalTok{(}
\NormalTok{            full\_race\_df3}\SpecialCharTok{$}\StringTok{"hispanic"} \SpecialCharTok{==} \DecValTok{1} \SpecialCharTok{\&}
\NormalTok{                full\_race\_df3}\SpecialCharTok{$}\StringTok{"mixed"} \SpecialCharTok{==} \DecValTok{0} \SpecialCharTok{\textasciitilde{}} \DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{,}
            \ConstantTok{TRUE} \SpecialCharTok{\textasciitilde{}} \DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{),}
        \AttributeTok{native\_american =}\NormalTok{ dplyr}\SpecialCharTok{::}\FunctionTok{case\_when}\NormalTok{(}
\NormalTok{            full\_race\_df3}\SpecialCharTok{$}\StringTok{"native\_american"} \SpecialCharTok{==} \DecValTok{1} \SpecialCharTok{\&}
\NormalTok{                full\_race\_df3}\SpecialCharTok{$}\StringTok{"mixed"} \SpecialCharTok{==} \DecValTok{0} \SpecialCharTok{\textasciitilde{}} \DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{,}
            \ConstantTok{TRUE} \SpecialCharTok{\textasciitilde{}} \DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{))}

\FunctionTok{colSums}\NormalTok{(full\_race\_df4 }\SpecialCharTok{|\textgreater{}} \FunctionTok{select}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialCharTok{{-}}\StringTok{"subjectkey"}\NormalTok{))}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
 asian_other_pi native_american           other              na           white 
              4               0              29               6             272 
          black        hispanic           mixed 
             32               3             108 
\end{verbatim}

Now the only categories with more than 5 people are other, white, black,
and mixed.

Moving forward, I see two sensible options:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Assign one race category per subject, where that category is either
  white, black, or other/mixed
\item
  Allow multiple race categories per subject, where those categories are
  white, black, hispanic, asian/other pacific islander, native american,
  and other
\end{enumerate}

Easier post-hoc analyses are provided by option 1, but more accurate
similarity matrices will be constructed with option 2. I unfortunately
can't think of any concrete reason as to why one of these two options
will give better SNF results, so switching between these two types will
need to be one of the free parameters in my upcoming SNF iterations. The
function \texttt{get\_race()} shown below is written to allow the user
to specify which format they want their dataframe in:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Option \#1 dummied (`condensed\_dummied'): three columns of white,
  black, mixed/other
\item
  Option \#1 undummied (`condensed\_undummied'): one race column with
  categories white, black, mixed/other
\item
  Option \#2 dummied (`expanded\_dummied'): multiple race columns
  including asian/other pacific islander, native american, other, white,
  black, hispanic
\end{enumerate}
